And then I’m trying to do my work and talk to people like everything’s normal, but it kind of just follows me through the whole day.\n\nI don’t know, I kept thinking maybe it was just being tired or needing a day off, but it’s been going on so long that it’s hard to ignore now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way most days?\n\nPatient: I guess… about two years now. A little more than that, actually—like two years and two months. It started around when the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I remember because everyone was complaining about the new shifts, and I kept thinking I’d get used to it, but… the low feeling just kind of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same things, I guess, but I don’t always think about whether I’m enjoying them or just doing them because that’s what I normally do.\n\nLike, I’ll put on a show or scroll on my phone, but then I end up thinking about work stuff, or whether I forgot to restock something in an exam room—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore.” It’s more just… everything feels kind of mixed together lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt genuinely happy or took pleasure in something, even for a short while?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, maybe there were little moments, but I don’t know if I’d call them genuinely happy. Like sometimes someone at work says something funny and I laugh, but then I’m right back to thinking about whatever I was doing.\n\nIt’s hard to separate, I guess. I don’t really sit there and think, “I’m enjoying this.” I just kind of keep moving through things. Like even if I’m watching something, I’ll realize I missed half of it because I was thinking about my schedule or some random errand I forgot. So… I don’t know. Nothing really stands out clearly.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your mood typically is from morning to night—does the low feeling stay with you most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Most days it’s kind of there from the start. Like I wake up and before I even do anything, it’s already this heavy, gray feeling on me. Then I’m getting ready, thinking about whether I’m going to be late or if the clinic is going to be backed up—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nIt doesn’t really come and go in a clean way. It pretty much follows me through the day, from morning to when I’m done with work and even after. Maybe it gets pushed to the side if I’m busy, but it doesn’t feel like it actually leaves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I sleep in or have a day off, it’s like my body still feels worn out. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. Then I’m getting ready, thinking about whether I’m going to be late or if the clinic is going to be backed up—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nIt doesn’t really come and go in a clean way. It pretty much follows me through the day, from morning to when I’m done with work and even after. Maybe it gets pushed to the side if I’m busy, but it doesn’t feel like it actually leaves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I sleep in or have a day off, it’s like my body still feels worn out. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. I mean, I came in because it’s been like this for a long time now—waking up already feeling low, like this heavy gray thing is sitting on me before I even get to the clinic.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing normal stuff, checking rooms or getting things ready, and I can kind of focus on tasks for a minute, but it doesn’t really go away. It just follows me through the day. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping recently, like waking up too early or having a hard time falling asleep, or on the flip side, have you found yourself sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, sleep is kind of… hard to answer. I mean, I go to bed, and sometimes I’m on my phone longer than I meant to be, but that’s also just because everybody does that, right? Like I’ll check one thing and then suddenly I’m watching some random video about cars or food or whatever.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything, so I can’t say for sure if I’m sleeping more or waking up too early. Some mornings I feel like I could stay in bed, but then I have work, so I just get up. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern, if that’s what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. I mean, I came in because it’s been like this for a long time now—waking up already feeling low, like this heavy gray thing is sitting on me before I even get to the clinic.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing normal stuff, checking rooms or getting things ready, and I can kind of focus on tasks for a minute, but it doesn’t really go away. It just follows me through the day. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping recently, like waking up too early or having a hard time falling asleep, or on the flip side, have you found yourself sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, sleep is kind of… hard to answer. I mean, I go to bed, and sometimes I’m on my phone longer than I meant to be, but that’s also just because everybody does that, right? Like I’ll check one thing and then suddenly I’m watching some random video about cars or food or whatever.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything, so I can’t say for sure if I’m sleeping more or waking up too early. Some mornings I feel like I could stay in bed, but then I have work, so I just get up. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you wake up earlier than usual and can't get back to sleep, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s earlier than usual exactly. Like sometimes there’s a truck outside or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m awake for a bit thinking about random stuff, like whether I need gas before work or if I put my scrubs in the wash.\n\nBut I haven’t been looking at the clock every time, so I can’t really say, like, “yeah, I wake up at 4 a.m. and can’t sleep.” It’s more just… I don’t know, mornings feel rough sometimes, but I can’t tell if that’s sleep or just me not wanting to get up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And then I’m trying to do my work and talk to people like everything’s normal, but it kind of just follows me through the whole day.\n\nI don’t know, I kept thinking maybe it was just being tired or needing a day off, but it’s been going on so long that it’s hard to ignore now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way most days?\n\nPatient: I guess… about two years now. A little more than that, actually—like two years and two months. It started around when the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I remember because everyone was complaining about the new shifts, and I kept thinking I’d get used to it, but… the low feeling just kind of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same things, I guess, but I don’t always think about whether I’m enjoying them or just doing them because that’s what I normally do.\n\nLike, I’ll put on a show or scroll on my phone, but then I end up thinking about work stuff, or whether I forgot to restock something in an exam room—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore.” It’s more just… everything feels kind of mixed together lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt genuinely happy or took pleasure in something, even for a short while?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, maybe there were little moments, but I don’t know if I’d call them genuinely happy. Like sometimes someone at work says something funny and I laugh, but then I’m right back to thinking about whatever I was doing.\n\nIt’s hard to separate, I guess. I don’t really sit there and think, “I’m enjoying this.” I just kind of keep moving through things. Like even if I’m watching something, I’ll realize I missed half of it because I was thinking about my schedule or some random errand I forgot. So… I don’t know. Nothing really stands out clearly.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your mood typically is from morning to night—does the low feeling stay with you most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Most days it’s kind of there from the start. Like I wake up and before I even do anything, it’s already this heavy, gray feeling on me. Then I’m getting ready, thinking about whether I’m going to be late or if the clinic is going to be backed up—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nIt doesn’t really come and go in a clean way. It pretty much follows me through the day, from morning to when I’m done with work and even after. Maybe it gets pushed to the side if I’m busy, but it doesn’t feel like it actually leaves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I sleep in or have a day off, it’s like my body still feels worn out. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. I mean, I came in because it’s been like this for a long time now—waking up already feeling low, like this heavy gray thing is sitting on me before I even get to the clinic.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing normal stuff, checking rooms or getting things ready, and I can kind of focus on tasks for a minute, but it doesn’t really go away. It just follows me through the day. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping recently, like waking up too early or having a hard time falling asleep, or on the flip side, have you found yourself sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, sleep is kind of… hard to answer. I mean, I go to bed, and sometimes I’m on my phone longer than I meant to be, but that’s also just because everybody does that, right? Like I’ll check one thing and then suddenly I’m watching some random video about cars or food or whatever.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything, so I can’t say for sure if I’m sleeping more or waking up too early. Some mornings I feel like I could stay in bed, but then I have work, so I just get up. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you wake up earlier than usual and can't get back to sleep, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s earlier than usual exactly. Like sometimes there’s a truck outside or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m awake for a bit thinking about random stuff, like whether I need gas before work or if I put my scrubs in the wash.\n\nBut I haven’t been looking at the clock every time, so I can’t really say, like, “yeah, I wake up at 4 a.m. and can’t sleep.” It’s more just… I don’t know, mornings feel rough sometimes, but I can’t tell if that’s sleep or just me not wanting to get up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re letting people down or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? Like, I wouldn’t say I’m walking around thinking, “I’m a terrible person” all day or anything. It’s more like, if something goes wrong, my brain kind of jumps to, “Did I mess that up?” even if it’s probably not totally on me.\n\nBut then again, at work, everybody’s stressed and short-staffed sometimes, so it’s hard to tell what’s me actually letting someone down versus just the whole day being a mess. Like yesterday the printer jammed and somehow that turned into three people waiting, and then I’m apologizing like I personally designed the printer. So… yeah, I guess I do that, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just how I am.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Um… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of something specific. Like, I used to look forward to stuff, I guess, but when you ask it like that my mind kind of goes blank.

It’s more like, if something goes wrong, my brain kind of jumps to, “Did I mess that up?” even if it’s probably not totally on me.\n\nBut then again, at work, everybody’s stressed and short-staffed sometimes, so it’s hard to tell what’s me actually letting someone down versus just the whole day being a mess. I’m not sure. Like, I know that’s probably a simple question, but I keep trying to pick something and then I’m like, “Was that ever actually enjoyable, or was it just something I did?”

I used to play games on my phone sometimes, or watch videos after work, but I don’t know if I enjoyed it in a real way or if it was just passing time. And then I’ll start one thing and end up doing something else, like checking if my schedule got posted or looking at shoes I’m not going to buy. So it’s hard to compare it to “how I used to.”

I guess I haven’t really noticed clearly. It’s more like days just kind of go by, but I don’t know if that means I’m not enjoying stuff or I’m just not thinking about it.

I remember because everyone was complaining about the new shifts, and I kept thinking I’d get used to it, but… the low feeling just kind of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same things, I guess, but I don’t always think about whether I’m enjoying them or just doing them because that’s what I normally do.\n\nLike, I’ll put on a show or scroll on my phone, but then I end up thinking about work stuff, or whether I forgot to restock something in an exam room—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore.” It’s more just… everything feels kind of mixed together lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt genuinely happy or took pleasure in something, even for a short while?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, maybe there were little moments, but I don’t know if I’d call them genuinely happy. Like sometimes someone at work says something funny and I laugh, but then I’m right back to thinking about whatever I was doing.\n\nIt’s hard to separate, I guess. I don’t really sit there and think, “I’m enjoying this.” I just kind of keep moving through things. Like even if I’m watching something, I’ll realize I missed half of it because I was thinking about my schedule or some random errand I forgot. So… I don’t know. Nothing really stands out clearly.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your mood typically is from morning to night—does the low feeling stay with you most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Most days it’s kind of there from the start. Like I wake up and before I even do anything, it’s already this heavy, gray feeling on me. Then I’m getting ready, thinking about whether I’m going to be late or if the clinic is going to be backed up—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nIt doesn’t really come and go in a clean way. It pretty much follows me through the day, from morning to when I’m done with work and even after. Maybe it gets pushed to the side if I’m busy, but it doesn’t feel like it actually leaves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I sleep in or have a day off, it’s like my body still feels worn out. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. I mean, I came in because it’s been like this for a long time now—waking up already feeling low, like this heavy gray thing is sitting on me before I even get to the clinic.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing normal stuff, checking rooms or getting things ready, and I can kind of focus on tasks for a minute, but it doesn’t really go away. It just follows me through the day. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping recently, like waking up too early or having a hard time falling asleep, or on the flip side, have you found yourself sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, sleep is kind of… hard to answer. I mean, I go to bed, and sometimes I’m on my phone longer than I meant to be, but that’s also just because everybody does that, right? Like I’ll check one thing and then suddenly I’m watching some random video about cars or food or whatever.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything, so I can’t say for sure if I’m sleeping more or waking up too early. Some mornings I feel like I could stay in bed, but then I have work, so I just get up. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you wake up earlier than usual and can't get back to sleep, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s earlier than usual exactly. Like sometimes there’s a truck outside or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m awake for a bit thinking about random stuff, like whether I need gas before work or if I put my scrubs in the wash.\n\nBut I haven’t been looking at the clock every time, so I can’t really say, like, “yeah, I wake up at 4 a.m. and can’t sleep.” It’s more just… I don’t know, mornings feel rough sometimes, but I can’t tell if that’s sleep or just me not wanting to get up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re letting people down or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? Like, I wouldn’t say I’m walking around thinking, “I’m a terrible person” all day or anything. It’s more like, if something goes wrong, my brain kind of jumps to, “Did I mess that up?” even if it’s probably not totally on me.\n\nBut then again, at work, everybody’s stressed and short-staffed sometimes, so it’s hard to tell what’s me actually letting someone down versus just the whole day being a mess. Like yesterday the printer jammed and somehow that turned into three people waiting, and then I’m apologizing like I personally designed the printer. So… yeah, I guess I do that, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just how I am.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of something you used to look forward to, even small things, and if so, do you still feel that anticipation or interest when you do it now?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of something specific. Like, I used to look forward to stuff, I guess, but when you ask it like that my mind kind of goes blank.\n\nI mean, I’ll still do little things, like watch something after work or get food I like, but I don’t really stop beforehand and think, “Oh yeah, I can’t wait for that.” It’s more just… it happens. But then sometimes if someone at work is talking about a show, I’ll look it up, and then I end up reading the cast list instead of actually watching it, which is kind of dumb.\n\nSo I’m not sure if that counts as not feeling anticipation, or if I’m just tired and distracted. I guess I haven’t really paid attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of something you used to enjoy doing, even if it was just a little thing, and tell me if you still enjoy it the way you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, I know that’s probably a simple question, but I keep trying to pick something and then I’m like, “Was that ever actually enjoyable, or was it just something I did?”\n\nI used to play games on my phone sometimes, or watch videos after work, but I don’t know if I enjoyed it in a real way or if it was just passing time. And then I’ll start one thing and end up doing something else, like checking if my schedule got posted or looking at shoes I’m not going to buy. So it’s hard to compare it to “how I used to.”\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed clearly. It’s more like days just kind of go by, but I don’t know if that means I’m not enjoying stuff or I’m just not thinking about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

  "candidates": ["F33.1", "F33.2", "F34.1"],
  "note": "",

Like, I wouldn’t say I’m walking around thinking, “I’m a terrible person” all day or anything. It’s more like, if something goes wrong, my brain kind of jumps to, “Did I mess that up?” even if it’s probably not totally on me.\n\nBut then again, at work, everybody’s stressed and short-staffed sometimes, so it’s hard to tell what’s me actually letting someone down versus just the whole day being a mess. Like yesterday the printer jammed and somehow that turned into three people waiting, and then I’m apologizing like I personally designed the printer. So… yeah, I guess I do that, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just how I am.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of something you used to look forward to, even small things, and if so, do you still feel that anticipation or interest when you do it now?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of something specific. Like, I used to look forward to stuff, I guess, but when you ask it like that my mind kind of goes blank.\n\nI mean, I’ll still do little things, like watch something after work or get food I like, but I don’t really stop beforehand and think, “Oh yeah, I can’t wait for that.” It’s more just… it happens. But then sometimes if someone at work is talking about a show, I’ll look it up, and then I end up reading the cast list instead of actually watching it, which is kind of dumb.\n\nSo I’m not sure if that counts as not feeling anticipation, or if I’m just tired and distracted. I guess I haven’t really paid attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of something you used to enjoy doing, even if it was just a little thing, and tell me if you still enjoy it the way you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, I know that’s probably a simple question, but I keep trying to pick something and then I’m like, “Was that ever actually enjoyable, or was it just something I did?”\n\nI used to play games on my phone sometimes, or watch videos after work, but I don’t know if I enjoyed it in a real way or if it was just passing time. And then I’ll start one thing and end up doing something else, like checking if my schedule got posted or looking at shoes I’m not going to buy.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… “not good enough” feels kind of big when you say it like that. I mean, I definitely second-guess myself, but I don’t know if I’d call it failing. Like at work, if I forget to restock something or someone asks where a chart is, I’ll be like, “Okay, great, I look incompetent,” but then two minutes later someone’s asking about lunch or where the gloves went, and it’s like… everything blends together.

I guess I don’t really sit there thinking, “I’m failing at life,” or anything. It’s more like small stuff piles up and I get annoyed with myself. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean or if you mean, like, a constant feeling. I haven’t really tracked it.

I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… I came in because I’ve just been feeling really low for a long time. Like, most mornings I wake up and it’s already there, this heavy gray feeling, before I even get to the clinic. And then I’m trying to do my work and talk to people like everything’s normal, but it kind of just follows me through the whole day.\n\nI don’t know, I kept thinking maybe it was just being tired or needing a day off, but it’s been going on so long that it’s hard to ignore now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way most days?\n\nPatient: I guess… about two years now. A little more than that, actually—like two years and two months. It started around when the staffing changed at the clinic and my schedule started rotating all over the place. I remember because everyone was complaining about the new shifts, and I kept thinking I’d get used to it, but… the low feeling just kind of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same things, I guess, but I don’t always think about whether I’m enjoying them or just doing them because that’s what I normally do.\n\nLike, I’ll put on a show or scroll on my phone, but then I end up thinking about work stuff, or whether I forgot to restock something in an exam room—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “Oh, I don’t like this anymore.” It’s more just… everything feels kind of mixed together lately.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time recently when you felt genuinely happy or took pleasure in something, even for a short while?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure. I mean, maybe there were little moments, but I don’t know if I’d call them genuinely happy. Like sometimes someone at work says something funny and I laugh, but then I’m right back to thinking about whatever I was doing.\n\nIt’s hard to separate, I guess. I don’t really sit there and think, “I’m enjoying this.” I just kind of keep moving through things. Like even if I’m watching something, I’ll realize I missed half of it because I was thinking about my schedule or some random errand I forgot. So… I don’t know. Nothing really stands out clearly.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your mood typically is from morning to night—does the low feeling stay with you most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Most days it’s kind of there from the start. Like I wake up and before I even do anything, it’s already this heavy, gray feeling on me. Then I’m getting ready, thinking about whether I’m going to be late or if the clinic is going to be backed up—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nIt doesn’t really come and go in a clean way. It pretty much follows me through the day, from morning to when I’m done with work and even after. Maybe it gets pushed to the side if I’m busy, but it doesn’t feel like it actually leaves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I sleep in or have a day off, it’s like my body still feels worn out. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. I mean, I came in because it’s been like this for a long time now—waking up already feeling low, like this heavy gray thing is sitting on me before I even get to the clinic.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing normal stuff, checking rooms or getting things ready, and I can kind of focus on tasks for a minute, but it doesn’t really go away. It just follows me through the day. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping recently, like waking up too early or having a hard time falling asleep, or on the flip side, have you found yourself sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, sleep is kind of… hard to answer. I mean, I go to bed, and sometimes I’m on my phone longer than I meant to be, but that’s also just because everybody does that, right? Like I’ll check one thing and then suddenly I’m watching some random video about cars or food or whatever.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything, so I can’t say for sure if I’m sleeping more or waking up too early. Some mornings I feel like I could stay in bed, but then I have work, so I just get up. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you wake up earlier than usual and can't get back to sleep, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s earlier than usual exactly. Like sometimes there’s a truck outside or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m awake for a bit thinking about random stuff, like whether I need gas before work or if I put my scrubs in the wash.\n\nBut I haven’t been looking at the clock every time, so I can’t really say, like, “yeah, I wake up at 4 a.m. and can’t sleep.” It’s more just… I don’t know, mornings feel rough sometimes, but I can’t tell if that’s sleep or just me not wanting to get up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re letting people down or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? Like, I wouldn’t say I’m walking around thinking, “I’m a terrible person” all day or anything. It’s more like, if something goes wrong, my brain kind of jumps to, “Did I mess that up?” even if it’s probably not totally on me.\n\nBut then again, at work, everybody’s stressed and short-staffed sometimes, so it’s hard to tell what’s me actually letting someone down versus just the whole day being a mess. I mean, maybe there were little moments, but I don’t know if I’d call them genuinely happy. Like sometimes someone at work says something funny and I laugh, but then I’m right back to thinking about whatever I was doing.\n\nIt’s hard to separate, I guess. I don’t really sit there and think, “I’m enjoying this.” I just kind of keep moving through things. Like even if I’m watching something, I’ll realize I missed half of it because I was thinking about my schedule or some random errand I forgot. So… I don’t know. Nothing really stands out clearly.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your mood typically is from morning to night—does the low feeling stay with you most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Most days it’s kind of there from the start. Like I wake up and before I even do anything, it’s already this heavy, gray feeling on me. Then I’m getting ready, thinking about whether I’m going to be late or if the clinic is going to be backed up—sorry, that’s not really the point.\n\nIt doesn’t really come and go in a clean way. It pretty much follows me through the day, from morning to when I’m done with work and even after. Maybe it gets pushed to the side if I’m busy, but it doesn’t feel like it actually leaves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Even if I sleep in or have a day off, it’s like my body still feels worn out. It’s not really fixed by resting.\n\nLike after work, after rooming patients and trying to catch up on charts—sometimes the charts get backed up because there’s always one room that needs something restocked, or someone needs vitals redone, and then I’m thinking about whether I documented it right—by the time I get home, I just feel emptied out. Like there’s nothing left for laundry or going to shoot hoops or even messing with my electronics stuff, which I usually can at least tinker with a little. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present most of the day, nearly every day, or are there significant periods when you don't feel this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s there most of the day. I mean, I came in because it’s been like this for a long time now—waking up already feeling low, like this heavy gray thing is sitting on me before I even get to the clinic.\n\nAnd then I’ll be doing normal stuff, checking rooms or getting things ready, and I can kind of focus on tasks for a minute, but it doesn’t really go away. It just follows me through the day. There aren’t really big stretches where I feel free from it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're physically slowed down? Or alternatively, have you been feeling guilty or worthless most days, even when things aren’t your fault?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower, like… I still do what I have to do at work, rooming patients and all that. Sometimes I probably talk quieter or take a second to answer, but I don’t know if that’s “slowed down” or just me being in my head.\n\nAnd the guilty or worthless part… I don’t know. I guess I can be hard on myself when I mess something up, like if I forget to restock something and then the next person has to deal with it, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking around saying that all day. I might need you to explain that one a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're not good enough or beat yourself up over small mistakes, even when it's not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say “often” exactly. Like if I mess up something small at work, I’ll think about it longer than I probably should—like, did I leave the cuff in the wrong room, did I forget to wipe something down, did someone else have to deal with it—and then I kind of replay it.\n\nBut then again, everybody does that a little, right? Like my supervisor will mention something and I’ll take it personally for a bit, even if she’s just saying it normally. I don’t know if that’s me not feeling good enough or just… I don’t know, trying not to mess up. I’m not really sure how to separate that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or think clearly lately, like you're having trouble making decisions or staying focused on tasks even when you're trying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s been happening. Like at work I’ll be looking at a patient’s intake screen and I’m just… staring at it, and I have to reread the same line over and over because it’s not sticking.\n\nAnd then I’ll realize I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll mix up what I was about to ask them next. Which is embarrassing because it’s literally my job to keep that stuff straight. Sometimes I’ll try to act normal and just double-check the chart again, but in my head I’m like, why can’t I just focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping recently, like waking up too early or having a hard time falling asleep, or on the flip side, have you found yourself sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t know, sleep is kind of… hard to answer. I mean, I go to bed, and sometimes I’m on my phone longer than I meant to be, but that’s also just because everybody does that, right? Like I’ll check one thing and then suddenly I’m watching some random video about cars or food or whatever.\n\nI haven’t really been tracking it or anything, so I can’t say for sure if I’m sleeping more or waking up too early. Some mornings I feel like I could stay in bed, but then I have work, so I just get up. I guess I haven’t noticed a clear pattern, if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you wake up earlier than usual and can't get back to sleep, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I do wake up sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s earlier than usual exactly. Like sometimes there’s a truck outside or my neighbor’s dog starts barking, and then I’m awake for a bit thinking about random stuff, like whether I need gas before work or if I put my scrubs in the wash.\n\nBut I haven’t been looking at the clock every time, so I can’t really say, like, “yeah, I wake up at 4 a.m. and can’t sleep.” It’s more just… I don’t know, mornings feel rough sometimes, but I can’t tell if that’s sleep or just me not wanting to get up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re letting people down or blaming yourself for things more than usual, even when it’s not entirely your fault?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe? Like, I wouldn’t say I’m walking around thinking, “I’m a terrible person” all day or anything. It’s more like, if something goes wrong, my brain kind of jumps to, “Did I mess that up?” even if it’s probably not totally on me.\n\nBut then again, at work, everybody’s stressed and short-staffed sometimes, so it’s hard to tell what’s me actually letting someone down versus just the whole day being a mess. Like yesterday the printer jammed and somehow that turned into three people waiting, and then I’m apologizing like I personally designed the printer. So… yeah, I guess I do that, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just how I am.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of something you used to look forward to, even small things, and if so, do you still feel that anticipation or interest when you do it now?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know. I’m trying to think of something specific. Like, I used to look forward to stuff, I guess, but when you ask it like that my mind kind of goes blank.\n\nI mean, I’ll still do little things, like watch something after work or get food I like, but I don’t really stop beforehand and think, “Oh yeah, I can’t wait for that.” It’s more just… it happens. But then sometimes if someone at work is talking about a show, I’ll look it up, and then I end up reading the cast list instead of actually watching it, which is kind of dumb.\n\nSo I’m not sure if that counts as not feeling anticipation, or if I’m just tired and distracted. I guess I haven’t really paid attention to it like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of something you used to enjoy doing, even if it was just a little thing, and tell me if you still enjoy it the way you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, I know that’s probably a simple question, but I keep trying to pick something and then I’m like, “Was that ever actually enjoyable, or was it just something I did?”\n\nI used to play games on my phone sometimes, or watch videos after work, but I don’t know if I enjoyed it in a real way or if it was just passing time. And then I’ll start one thing and end up doing something else, like checking if my schedule got posted or looking at shoes I’m not going to buy. So it’s hard to compare it to “how I used to.”\n\nI guess I haven’t really noticed clearly. It’s more like days just kind of go by, but I don’t know if that means I’m not enjoying stuff or I’m just not thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling like you're not good enough, or that you're failing even when you're trying your best?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “not good enough” feels kind of big when you say it like that. I mean, I definitely second-guess myself, but I don’t know if I’d call it failing. Like at work, if I forget to restock something or someone asks where a chart is, I’ll be like, “Okay, great, I look incompetent,” but then two minutes later someone’s asking about lunch or where the gloves went, and it’s like… everything blends together.\n\nI guess I don’t really sit there thinking, “I’m failing at life,” or anything. It’s more like small stuff piles up and I get annoyed with myself. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean or if you mean, like, a constant feeling. I haven’t really tracked it.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to think clearly or make decisions lately, even about simple things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not like I can’t think at all, but at work I’ll be on a patient’s intake screen and I’ll just be staring at the same line, rereading it like three or four times, and it still doesn’t really go in.\n\nAnd then I’ll notice I missed some little detail, like a medication dose, or I’ll be about to ask the next intake question and then my brain just kind of… switches channels. Like I know I was about to ask something, but it’s gone. Then I’m trying to act normal while I find my place again.\n\nIt’s annoying because the screens all look the same after a while, and sometimes people are talking nearby about random stuff, like weekend plans or whatever, and I’ll realize I’m listening to that instead of the patient in front of me. So yeah, simple things feel harder to keep straight lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you've lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. Like, I guess I still do some of the same stuff, but I can’t tell if I’m enjoying it or if it’s just… something to do because it’s there.\n\nLike I’ll open a video or a game or whatever, and then five minutes later I’m checking something else, or I’m just letting it run. But then sometimes everyone does that, right? Like my cousin will have three apps open and say he’s “relaxing,” so I don’t know if that means anything.\n\nI haven’t had this clear moment where I’m like, “Oh, I don’t like anything anymore.” It’s more just… I’m not sure what I actually like versus what I’m used to doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

      "Absence of manic or hypomanic episodes confirmed",
      "Symptoms not better explained by adjustment disorder",
      "No evidence of psychotic features"
    ]
  }
}
